This\nfeature provides it as a function in `internal/csvdata` so no other package re-implements it.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `func IsNull(value string) bool` in package `csvdata` receives a value that has already been\n  trimmed of leading and trailing spaces.\n- A value is null when it is the empty string, or equals `NA`, `N/A`, or `NULL` ignoring case\n  (`na`, `n/a`, `Null` and `null` are null).\n- Every other value is non-null, including `0`, `false`, `NAN`, `N/B`, `NULLS` and `NA ` is not\n  an input (trimming precedes this rule), so only exact spellings match.\n- A quoted empty field (`\"\"`) is read by `encoding/csv` as an empty string, so it is null.\n- A field made only of spaces is empty after trimming and is therefore null.\n- `IsNull` is pure: it never reads files and never allocates state.\n\n## Tests the build must write\n\nThe project's own suite in `internal/csvdata` contains these test functions (names are fixed so\nacceptance can run them):\n\n- `TestIsNullEmpty`\n- `TestIsNullSpellings`\n- `TestIsNullNonNullValues`\n- `TestQuotedEmptyFieldIsNull`\n- `TestSpacesOnlyFieldIsNull`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC null-empty ===\nIntent: The empty string is reported null by csvdata.IsNull.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvdata\", \"TestIsNullEmpty\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC null-empty ===\n\n=== AC null-spellings ===\nIntent: NA, N/A and NULL are null in any letter case.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvdata\", \"TestIsNullSpellings\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC null-spellings ===\n\n=== AC null-non-null-values ===\nIntent: Ordinary values such as 0, false and near-miss spellings are not null.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvdata\", \"TestIsNullNonNullValues\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC null-non-null-values ===\n\n=== AC null-quoted-empty ===\nIntent: A quoted empty field read from a CSV file is null.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvdata\", \"TestQuotedEmptyFieldIsNull\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC null-quoted-empty ===\n\n=== AC null-spaces-only ===\nIntent: A field of only spaces is null once trimmed.\nRequires: executable=go; no other token is treated as null.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-InputRejection.md ===\n# FEATURE: Input Rejection\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Reject empty files, blank-only files, empty or repeated header names, and ragged records with exit 3. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-Trimming.md, FEATURE-CsvReading.md, FEATURE-Arguments.md |\n| Provides    | csvdata.ErrRejected |\n| Consumes    | csvdata.Read, cli.ParseArgs |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nMalformed input must never produce partial output. Rejection is detected in `internal/csvdata`\nand mapped to exit code 3 by `internal/cli`.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `csvdata` exposes a rejection error type (sentinel or typed error matched with `errors.Is` /\n  `errors.As`) named `ErrRejected`. For a ragged record it carries the 1-based line number.\n- The file is rejected when:\n  - it is empty (zero bytes);\n  - it contains only blank lines;\n  - any header name is empty after trimming;\n  - any header name repeats (compared exactly after trimming);\n  - any record has a different number of fields from the header.\n- A header-only file is valid and yields zero data records.\n- A trailing line ending after the last record creates no extra record.\n- `cli.Run` reads the file named by the parsed arguments. On a rejection error it writes nothing\n  to standard output, writes one diagnostic line to standard error naming the file (and the line\n  number for a ragged record), and returns exit code 3.\n- A CSV syntax error from `encoding/csv` (for example a bare quote) is also a rejection, exit 3.\n\n## Tests the build must write\n\nIn `internal/csvdata`:\n\n- `TestRejectEmptyFile`\n- `TestRejectBlankLinesOnly`\n- `TestRejectEmptyHeaderName`\n- `TestRejectRepeatedHeaderName`\n- `TestRejectRaggedRecord`\n- `TestHeaderOnlyFileAccepted`\n- `TestTrailingNewlineAddsNoRecord`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC reject-unit-empty-and-blank ===\nIntent: Empty files and files of only blank lines are rejected by the reader.\nRequires: executable=go; This feature starts `profile.InferType` in\n`internal/profile` with the two numeric types. Later features extend the same function with\n`date`, `boolean`, `text`, and `empty`.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `func InferType(values []string) string` in package `profile` receives the trimmed values of one\n  column and returns the type name.\n- Null values (per `csvdata.IsNull`) are ignored.\n- A column is `integer` when every non-null value matches `-?[0-9]+`.\n- Otherwise it is `number` when every non-null value matches `-?[0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?`.\n- Integer is tested before number, so a column of whole numbers is `integer`; a single decimal\n  value makes the column `number`.\n- Matching is anchored over the whole value and uses no float parsing. Values such as `+1`, `1.`,\n  `.5`, `1e3`, `1,5`, `0x10`, and `-` are not numeric.\n- When a column has at least one non-null value that is not numeric, this feature returns the\n  fallback `text` until later features add the remaining types.\n- A column whose non-null values are all null returns `empty`.\n\n## Tests the build must write\n\nIn `internal/profile`:\n\n- `TestInferIntegerColumn`\n- `TestInferNumberColumn`\n- `TestIntegerPreferredOverNumber`\n- `TestNullsIgnoredWhenInferringNumeric`\n- `TestMalformedNumericsAreNotNumeric`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC numeric-integer ===\nIntent: A column of integer-shaped values, including negatives, is typed integer.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestInferIntegerColumn\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC numeric-integer ===\n\n=== AC numeric-number ===\nIntent: A column with a decimal value among integers is typed number.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestInferNumberColumn\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC numeric-number ===\n\n=== AC numeric-integer-preferred ===\nIntent: Integer is chosen over number when both patterns match every value.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestIntegerPreferredOverNumber\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC numeric-integer-preferred ===\n\n=== AC numeric-nulls-ignored ===\nIntent: Null spellings among numeric values do not change the inferred type.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestNullsIgnoredWhenInferringNumeric\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC numeric-nulls-ignored ===\n\n=== AC numeric-malformed ===\nIntent: Values such as +1, 1., .5, 1e3 and 1,5 are not numeric.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestMalformedNumericsAreNotNumeric\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC numeric-malformed ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Classification never uses float parsing; values stay exact strings for later exact arithmetic.\n- No type is hardcoded for any column or file; every type comes from the values examined.\n- Only the standard library is used.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-DateType.md ===\n# FEATURE: Date Type\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Infer date columns whose non-null values are real YYYY-MM-DD calendar dates. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-NumericTypes.md |\n| Provides    | profile.InferType (date) |\n| Consumes    | csvdata.IsNull, profile.InferType |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nExtend `profile.InferType` so date columns are recognised and sort chronologically in later\nstatistics.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- After integer and number are ruled out, a column is `date` when every non-null value has the\n  shape `YYYY-MM-DD` (four digits, hyphen, two digits, hyphen, two digits) and is a real calendar\n  date.\n- Real-date validation uses `time.Parse` with layout `2006-01-02` in addition to the digit-shape\n  check, so `2025-02-30`, `2025-13-01`, and `2025-02-29` are not dates while `2024-02-29` is.\n- Shapes such as `2025-2-3`, `2025/02/03`, `20250203`, and `2025-02-03T10:00` are not dates.\n- Null values are ignored.\n- One non-date value makes the column fall through to later types (`boolean`, then `text`).\n  Integer and number take precedence, but no YYYY-MM-DD value can match them.\n\n## Tests the build must write\n\nIn `internal/profile`:\n\n- `TestInferDateColumn`\n- `TestInvalidCalendarDateIsNotDate`\n- `TestLeapDayHandling`\n- `TestDateShapeIsStrict`\n- `TestNullsIgnoredWhenInferringDate`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC date-column ===\nIntent: A column of valid YYYY-MM-DD values is typed date.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestInferDateColumn\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC date-column ===\n\n=== AC date-invalid-calendar ===\nIntent: A column containing an impossible date such as 2025-02-30 is not typed date.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestInvalidCalendarDateIsNotDate\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC date-invalid-calendar ===\n\n=== AC date-leap-day ===\nIntent: A leap-year February 29 is a date and a non-leap-year February 29 is not.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestLeapDayHandling\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC date-leap-day ===\n\n=== AC date-shape-strict ===\nIntent: Values with the wrong shape (unpadded, slashes, timestamps) are not dates.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestDateShapeIsStrict\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC date-shape-strict ===\n\n=== AC date-nulls-ignored ===\nIntent: Null spellings among dates do not change the inferred date type.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestNullsIgnoredWhenInferringDate\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC date-nulls-ignored ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Date validity is computed from the value, never from a list of known dates.\n- Only the standard library (`time`, `regexp`) is used.\n- The numeric rules from FEATURE-NumericTypes.md are not altered.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-BooleanTextTypes.md ===\n# FEATURE: Boolean and Text Types\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Infer boolean, text, and empty column types, completing the type order. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-DateType.md |\n| Provides    | profile.InferType (boolean, text, empty) |\n| Consumes    | csvdata.IsNull, profile.InferType |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nComplete `profile.InferType` so every column receives exactly one type: the first of `integer`,\n`number`, `date`, `boolean`, `text` that every non-null value satisfies, or `empty` when there is\nno non-null value.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- A column is `boolean` when every non-null value equals `true` or `false` ignoring case\n  (`True`, `TRUE`, `false`, `False`). Values such as `yes`, `1`, `0`, and `t` are not boolean.\n- `text` is the fallback for any column that satisfies none of the earlier types.\n- A column with no non-null value (every value null, or zero data rows) is `empty`.\n- The order of evaluation is: empty check, integer, number, date, boolean, text.\n- A column of `0` and `1` is `integer`, not boolean.\n- Original value case is preserved for distinct counting; only classification ignores case. The\n  min and max statistics later render booleans as lowercase `true` or `false` regardless of input\n  case; that rendering belongs to the min/max feature.\n- Null values are ignored during classification.\n\n## Tests the build must write\n\nIn `internal/profile`:\n\n- `TestInferBooleanColumnMixedCase`\n- `TestInferTextFallback`\n- `TestInferEmptyWhenAllNull`\n- `TestInferEmptyWhenNoRows`\n- `TestBooleanLookalikesAreText`\n- `TestTypeOrderPrecedence`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC type-boolean-mixed-case ===\nIntent: Values true and false in any letter case form a boolean column.\nRequires: executable=go; BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-NullValues.md ===\n# FEATURE: Null Values\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Recognise null spellings (empty, NA, N/A, NULL ignoring case) on trimmed values. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-Trimming.md, FEATURE-CsvReading.md |\n| Provides    | csvdata.IsNull |\n| Consumes    | csvdata.Read |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nStatistics, type inference and frequency counting all need one shared definition of \"null\". This\nfeature provides it as a function in `internal/csvdata` so no other package re-implements it.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `func IsNull(value string) bool` in package `csvdata` receives a value that has already been\n  trimmed of leading and trailing spaces.\n- A value is null when it is the empty string, or equals `NA`, `N/A`, or `NULL` ignoring case\n  (`na`, `n/a`, `Null` and `null` are null).\n- Every other value is non-null, including `0`, `false`, `NAN`, `N/B`, `NULLS` and `NA ` is not\n  an input (trimming precedes this rule), so only exact spellings match.\n- A quoted empty field (`\"\"`) is read by `encoding/csv` as an empty string, so it is null.\n- A field made only of spaces is empty after trimming and is therefore null.\n- `IsNull` is pure: it never reads files and never allocates state.\n\n## Tests the build must write\n\nThe project's own suite in `internal/csvdata` contains these test functions (names are fixed so\nacceptance can run them):\n\n- `TestIsNullEmpty`\n- `TestIsNullSpellings`\n- `TestIsNullNonNullValues`\n- `TestQuotedEmptyFieldIsNull`\n- `TestSpacesOnlyFieldIsNull`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC null-empty ===\nIntent: The empty string is reported null by csvdata.IsNull.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvdata\", \"TestIsNullSpellings\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC null-spellings ===\n\n=== AC null-non-null-values ===\nIntent: Ordinary values such as 0, false and near-miss spellings are not null.\nRequires: executable=go; no other token is treated as null.\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-InputRejection.md ===\n# FEATURE: Input Rejection\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Reject empty files, blank-only files, empty or repeated header names, and ragged records with exit 3. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-Trimming.md, FEATURE-CsvReading.md, FEATURE-Arguments.md |\n| Provides    | csvdata.ErrRejected |\n| Consumes    | csvdata.Read, cli.ParseArgs |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nMalformed input must never produce partial output. Rejection is detected in `internal/csvdata`\nand mapped to exit code 3 by `internal/cli`.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `csvdata` exposes a rejection error type (sentinel or typed error matched with `errors.Is` /\n  `errors.As`) named `ErrRejected`. For a ragged record it carries the 1-based line number.\n- The file is rejected when:\n  - it is empty (zero bytes);\n  - it contains only blank lines;\n  - any header name is empty after trimming;\n  - any header name repeats (compared exactly after trimming);\n  - any record has a different number of fields from the header.\n- A header-only file is valid and yields zero data records.\n- A trailing line ending after the last record creates no extra record.\n- `cli.Run` reads the file named by the parsed arguments. On a rejection error it writes nothing\n  to standard output, writes one diagnostic line to standard error naming the file (and the line\n  number for a ragged record), and returns exit code 3.\n- A CSV syntax error from `encoding/csv` (for example a bare quote) is also a rejection, exit 3.\n\n## Tests the build must write\n\nIn `internal/csvdata`:\n\n- `TestRejectEmptyFile`\n- `TestRejectBlankLinesOnly`\n- `TestRejectEmptyHeaderName`\n- `TestRejectRepeatedHeaderName`\n- `TestRejectRaggedRecord`\n- `TestHeaderOnlyFileAccepted`\n- `TestTrailingNewlineAddsNoRecord`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC reject-unit-empty-and-blank ===\nIntent: Empty files and files of only blank lines are rejected by the reader.\nRequires: executable=go; This feature starts `profile.InferType` in\n`internal/profile` with the two numeric types. Later features extend the same function with\n`date`, `boolean`, `text`, and `empty`.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- `func InferType(values []string) string` in package `profile` receives the trimmed values of one\n  column and returns the type name.\n- Null values (per `csvdata.IsNull`) are ignored.\n- A column is `integer` when every non-null value matches `-?[0-9]+`.\n- Otherwise it is `number` when every non-null value matches `-?[0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?`.\n- Integer is tested before number, so a column of whole numbers is `integer`; a single decimal\n  value makes the column `number`.\n- Matching is anchored over the whole value and uses no float parsing. Values such as `+1`, `1.`,\n  `.5`, `1e3`, `1,5`, `0x10`, and `-` are not numeric.\n- When a column has at least one non-null value that is not numeric, this feature returns the\n  fallback `text` until later features add the remaining types.\n- A column whose non-null values are all null returns `empty`.\n\n## Tests the build must write\n\nIn `internal/profile`:\n\n- `TestInferIntegerColumn`\n- `TestInferNumberColumn`\n- `TestIntegerPreferredOverNumber`\n- `TestNullsIgnoredWhenInferringNumeric`\n- `TestMalformedNumericsAreNotNumeric`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC numeric-integer ===\nIntent: A column of integer-shaped values, including negatives, is typed integer.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestIntegerPreferredOverNumber\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC numeric-integer-preferred ===\n\n=== AC numeric-nulls-ignored ===\nIntent: Null spellings among numeric values do not change the inferred type.\nRequires: executable=go; Values such as `yes`, `1`, `0`, and `t` are not boolean.\n- `text` is the fallback for any column that satisfies none of the earlier types.\n- A column with no non-null value (every value null, or zero data rows) is `empty`.\n- The order of evaluation is: empty check, integer, number, date, boolean, text.\n- A column of `0` and `1` is `integer`, not boolean.\n- Original value case is preserved for distinct counting; only classification ignores case. The\n  min and max statistics later render booleans as lowercase `true` or `false` regardless of input\n  case; that rendering belongs to the min/max feature.\n- Null values are ignored during classification.\n\n## Tests the build must write\n\nIn `internal/profile`:\n\n- `TestInferBooleanColumnMixedCase`\n- `TestInferTextFallback`\n- `TestInferEmptyWhenAllNull`\n- `TestInferEmptyWhenNoRows`\n- `TestBooleanLookalikesAreText`\n- `TestTypeOrderPrecedence`\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC type-boolean-mixed-case ===\nIntent: Values true and false in any letter case form a boolean column.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/profile\", \"TestInferBooleanColumnMixedCase\")\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC type-boolean-mixed-case ===\n\n=== AC type-text-fallback ===\nIntent: A column matching no earlier type is text.\nRequires: executable=go;
